I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that it scares me a little.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it’s not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I’ll second-guess myself afterward and wonder if I handled things wrong, but I don’t know that I’d call it guilt exactly.\n\nMaybe I’m not answering that right. I haven’t really noticed blaming myself for things that weren’t my fault, at least not more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually upbeat, energized, or overly confident, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like unusually upbeat or overly confident. I wish I had moments like that, honestly.\n\nI mean, I can have times where I’m trying to act okay or push through, but I wouldn’t describe it as feeling energized in a good way. I don’t think that’s been happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or without interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, more days than not, even if just slightly?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think it’s been most of the day, more days than not. I’ve had moments where I feel kind of flat or just not myself, but I wouldn’t want to say I’m sad all the time or that I don’t enjoy anything.\n\nMaybe I’m downplaying it a little, but… I don’t think it fits the way you’re asking. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been dealing with any major stress or life changes recently that might be contributing to how you’ve been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there has been. It’s been about two weeks now, since the kitchen fire during our first weekend working on the house.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic, but that’s really when all of this started feeling different. Does that count as a major stressor?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I’ve been getting really short with people, more than I’d like to admit. I don’t usually have a temper, or at least I try not to, but I kind of blew up at one of my employees over something small—stock being put on the wrong shelf. I yelled, and afterward I was shaking because… that’s just not how I normally treat people. I feel pretty awful about it.\n\nDoctor: Has this kind of emotional outburst happened before, or was this the first time you reacted this way?\n\nPatient: It’s… not exactly the first time, no. I wish I could say it was just that one moment, but I’ve been snapping at people over really small things lately. I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic—I’m not trying to be awful—but I keep blowing up, and then afterward I feel shaky and embarrassed because that’s really not how I usually am with people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’m depressed, exactly. I’ve just been kind of off, maybe not as excited about things, but I haven’t really been tracking it. I’m sorry, I’m not sure if that’s the kind of answer you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself getting angry or irritable even when there’s no clear reason for it, like something small or even nothing at all setting you off?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out of control all the time, but lately it really has been small things setting me off. Like with my employee putting stock on the wrong shelf—I yelled, and it was… it was way more than the situation called for. Afterward I was shaking, because that’s just not how I usually treat people.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve been feeling more irritable or angry most days, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I don’t want to make myself sound like an angry person, because I’m really not, or at least I don’t think I am. But lately I keep blowing up over things that shouldn’t matter that much. Like I yelled at one of my employees just for putting stock on the wrong shelf, and afterward I was shaking because… that’s just not how I usually treat people.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how active you feel from day to day?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear change, I guess. Some days I feel kind of worn out, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it a change in my energy. Is that okay? Like, I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just haven’t been paying close attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel irritable or angry throughout the day, even when nothing specific is happening?\n\nPatient: It’s… honestly, it feels like it comes up a lot during the day, even when I can’t point to one real reason. I try to keep it together, especially at work, but then something tiny happens and I just snap.\n\nLike I mentioned, I yelled at one of my employees for putting stock on the wrong shelf, and afterward I was shaking because that’s really not how I usually treat people. I don’t want to sound like I’m just angry all the time, but it’s been happening enough that it’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if these feelings of irritability and anger tend to come in waves, or do they seem to build up over time before you snap?\n\nPatient: It feels more like it builds up, I think. I try to act normal and not make a big deal out of it, but then something small happens and I just… overreact. Like with my employee and the stock being on the wrong shelf — I yelled, and afterward I was shaking because that’s really not me. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that it scares me a little.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it’s not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I’ll second-guess myself afterward and wonder if I handled things wrong, but I don’t know that I’d call it guilt exactly.\n\nMaybe I’m not answering that right. I haven’t really noticed blaming myself for things that weren’t my fault, at least not more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually upbeat, energized, or overly confident, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like unusually upbeat or overly confident. I wish I had moments like that, honestly.\n\nI mean, I can have times where I’m trying to act okay or push through, but I wouldn’t describe it as feeling energized in a good way. I don’t think that’s been happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or without interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, more days than not, even if just slightly?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think it’s been most of the day, more days than not. I’ve had moments where I feel kind of flat or just not myself, but I wouldn’t want to say I’m sad all the time or that I don’t enjoy anything.\n\nMaybe I’m downplaying it a little, but… I don’t think it fits the way you’re asking. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been dealing with any major stress or life changes recently that might be contributing to how you’ve been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there has been. It’s been about two weeks now, since the kitchen fire during our first weekend working on the house.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic, but that’s really when all of this started feeling different. Does that count as a major stressor?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or uninterested in things you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not nearly every day for two whole weeks. I’ve had times where I’m kind of low or not really excited about things, but I don’t know that it’s been most of the day like that.\n\nI don’t want to answer wrong, but I think the honest answer is no… or at least, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you take less pleasure or interest in activities you usually enjoy, even if just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not totally sure. I still do things I normally like, and sometimes they feel okay… just maybe not as easy to get into as usual.\n\nI wouldn’t want to overstate it. It was… it was really scary. I mean, I know it could’ve been worse, and I’m okay, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic.

Yeah… I guess I have been, more than I wanted to admit.

Like, I’m not trying to avoid the question—I just haven’t been paying close attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel irritable or angry throughout the day, even when nothing specific is happening?\n\nPatient: It’s… honestly, it feels like it comes up a lot during the day, even when I can’t point to one real reason. I try to keep it together, especially at work, but then something tiny happens and I just snap.\n\nLike I mentioned, I yelled at one of my employees for putting stock on the wrong shelf, and afterward I was shaking because that’s really not how I usually treat people. I don’t want to sound like I’m just angry all the time, but it’s been happening enough that it’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if these feelings of irritability and anger tend to come in waves, or do they seem to build up over time before you snap?\n\nPatient: It feels more like it builds up, I think. I try to act normal and not make a big deal out of it, but then something small happens and I just… overreact. Like with my employee and the stock being on the wrong shelf — I yelled, and afterward I was shaking because that’s really not me. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that it scares me a little.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it’s not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I’ll second-guess myself afterward and wonder if I handled things wrong, but I don’t know that I’d call it guilt exactly.\n\nMaybe I’m not answering that right. I haven’t really noticed blaming myself for things that weren’t my fault, at least not more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually upbeat, energized, or overly confident, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like unusually upbeat or overly confident. I wish I had moments like that, honestly.\n\nI mean, I can have times where I’m trying to act okay or push through, but I wouldn’t describe it as feeling energized in a good way. I don’t think that’s been happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or without interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, more days than not, even if just slightly?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think it’s been most of the day, more days than not. I’ve had moments where I feel kind of flat or just not myself, but I wouldn’t want to say I’m sad all the time or that I don’t enjoy anything.\n\nMaybe I’m downplaying it a little, but… I don’t think it fits the way you’re asking. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been dealing with any major stress or life changes recently that might be contributing to how you’ve been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there has been. It’s been about two weeks now, since the kitchen fire during our first weekend working on the house.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic, but that’s really when all of this started feeling different. Does that count as a major stressor?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or uninterested in things you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not nearly every day for two whole weeks. I’ve had times where I’m kind of low or not really excited about things, but I don’t know that it’s been most of the day like that.\n\nI don’t want to answer wrong, but I think the honest answer is no… or at least, not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you take less pleasure or interest in activities you usually enjoy, even if just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not totally sure. I still do things I normally like, and sometimes they feel okay… just maybe not as easy to get into as usual.\n\nI wouldn’t want to overstate it. I’ve noticed I’m kind of distracted, I guess, but I don’t know if that counts as less interest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re getting less enjoyment or pleasure out of the things you usually like to do, even if just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say less enjoyment exactly. Maybe I’m a little distracted when I’m doing things, but I can still enjoy them some.\n\nSo… I don’t think it’s a big change, if that makes sense. I just don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped liking everything, because I haven’t.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the kitchen fire and how it affected you emotionally at the time?\n\nPatient: It was… it was really scary. I mean, I know it could’ve been worse, and I’m okay, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic.\n\nBut at the time I just froze emotionally, I think. And since then it sort of keeps popping into my head when I’m not expecting it. Like I’ll pick up a pan, or even be doing something totally unrelated like opening a spreadsheet at work, and suddenly it’s like I’m right back there—smelling smoke and seeing the kitchen fill up. It comes in before I can really stop it.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean, but… yeah, that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Since the kitchen fire, have you had repeated, unwanted memories or flashbacks of the event that come to mind even when you don’t want them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it does kind of jump into my head when I’m not trying to think about it.\n\nLike I’ll pick up a pan, or even just be doing something normal like opening a spreadsheet, and suddenly it feels like I’m back there—smelling the smoke and seeing the kitchen filling up. It happens before I can really stop it. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Since the kitchen fire, have you been avoiding certain things—like places, people, or activities—that remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been, more than I wanted to admit.\n\nI’ve been avoiding the house when I can, and even stupid little things like the mortgage folder because it’s tied to everything from that day. And I have herbs out on the patio that I used to like taking care of, but now even seeing them makes my chest tighten, so I just… don’t go near them.\n\nI know that probably sounds kind of silly, but it’s like anything connected to that day, my body reacts before I can talk myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Since the kitchen fire, have you found yourself being more easily startled or more alert than usual, like you’re always on edge or watching for danger?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, unfortunately. I don’t mean to be jumpy on purpose or anything, but at work if a store alarm beeps or a pallet jack bangs behind me, I jump really hard—like I almost drop whatever I’m holding.\n\nAnd then everyone else just kind of goes back to what they were doing, but my heart keeps pounding for a while after. I feel a little embarrassed saying that, but… yes, I guess I’ve been more on edge than usual."
